```
     1→# Plan: Evolve Agent Interface for Contributor-Ready Plugin Surface
     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→The CLI is now open source and receiving contributions. The current agent integration surface area requires contributors to:
     6→1. Implement a 17-method `Agent` interface with unclear required-vs-optional boundaries
     7→2. Write per-agent handler functions in the `cli` package (due to import cycles)
     8→3. Register handlers via stringly-typed `(agentName, hookName)` pairs in `hook_registry.go`
     9→4. Understand implicit mappings from agent-native hooks to lifecycle semantics
    10→5. Duplicate framework logic (state transitions, strategy calls, file detection) in each handler
    11→
    12→This plan redesigns the agent interface around explicit lifecycle events with a generic framework dispatcher, making it compile-time safe and contributor-friendly.
    13→
    14→## Design Rationale
    15→
    16→### The core insight: agents are data providers, not orchestrators
    17→
    18→The current architecture grew organically as we added agents. Each agent's handler file (`hooks_claudecode_handlers.go`, `hooks_geminicli_handlers.go`) contains a mix of agent-specific parsing and framework orchestration logic — state transitions, strategy calls, file change detection, context generation. When a contributor wants to add a new agent, they have to understand and reproduce all of that framework machinery, which is both error-prone and creates maintenance burden as the framework evolves.
    19→
    20→The key realization is that agents should be **passive data providers**. They know how to parse their own hook inputs and transcript formats, but they should never drive the checkpoint/step lifecycle themselves. The framework should call *into* the agent for data (transcript bytes, modified file lists, prompts, summaries) and handle all the orchestration — state machine transitions, strategy method calls, file change detection, metadata generation — in one place.
    21→
    22→This inverts the current flow from "agent handler calls framework functions" to "framework dispatcher calls agent methods."
    23→
    24→### Separating Events from Actions
    25→
    26→We initially conflated what happens (events) with what the framework does about it (actions). For example, "StepSave" was initially proposed as an event type, but it's actually a framework *response* to a TurnEnd event. Getting this distinction right was critical:
    27→
    28→- **Events** are inputs — things that happen in the outside world. They come from agents (SessionStart, TurnStart, TurnEnd, Compaction, SessionEnd, SubagentStart, SubagentEnd) or from git (GitCommit, PrePush). An agent's only job is to translate its native hooks into these normalized events.
    29→- **Actions** are outputs — what the framework does in response. StepSave, Checkpoint, Condense, PhaseTransition, ResetTranscriptOffset. These are entirely internal to the framework and invisible to agent implementers.
    30→
    31→This means a contributor adding a new agent only needs to think about: "which of my hooks correspond to which lifecycle events?" They never need to understand the action dispatch machinery.
    32→
    33→### ParseHookEvent as the contribution surface
    34→
    35→The most important method in the new interface is `ParseHookEvent(hookName string, stdin io.Reader) (*Event, error)`. This is where 90% of agent-specific work lives. The agent reads its native hook payload from stdin, extracts the session ID, transcript path, and any other relevant data, and returns a normalized `Event` — or `nil` if the hook has no lifecycle significance.
    36→
    37→This design handles the "pass-through hook" problem elegantly. Agents like Gemini have hooks (before-tool, after-tool, before-model, etc.) that we acknowledge but don't act on. Rather than requiring dummy handler registrations for each, the agent simply returns `nil` from `ParseHookEvent` and the dispatcher does nothing.
    38→
    39→### Why transcript operations are core, not optional
    40→
    41→We initially considered making transcript reading/chunking/analysis optional interfaces, since not all hypothetical future agents might have accessible transcripts. But transcript data is fundamental to the product — it's what gets stored in checkpoints, what drives the "files modified" detection, and what enables session logs and summaries. An agent that can't provide transcript access can't meaningfully participate in the checkpoint lifecycle. Making these methods core forces contributors to think about transcript access upfront rather than discovering the gap later.
    42→
    43→### Step vs Checkpoint terminology
    44→
    45→The codebase had been using "checkpoint" loosely for two distinct concepts: the temporary per-turn saves on shadow branches and the permanent persisted saves on `entire/checkpoints/v1`. We established precise terminology:
    46→- A **Step** is saved to the shadow branch — it's temporary, per-turn, and gets cleaned up on condensation
    47→- A **Checkpoint** is persisted to `entire/checkpoints/v1` — it's permanent and survives branch operations
    48→
    49→This distinction matters because both happen on TurnEnd (the framework saves a step *and* writes a checkpoint), but they have different lifecycles and storage mechanisms. The strategy interface rename (`SaveChanges` → `SaveStep`, `SaveTaskCheckpoint` → `SaveTaskStep`) makes the code self-documenting.
    50→
    51→### Compaction and session handoff
    52→
    53→Two lifecycle events we identified during design that weren't previously modeled:
    54→
    55→**Compaction** occurs when an agent compresses its context window (e.g., Gemini's pre-compress hook). This triggers the same save logic as TurnEnd but also resets the transcript offset, since the transcript may be truncated or reorganized. We also added defensive detection: if `GetTranscriptPosition()` returns a value smaller than the stored offset, the framework treats it as an implicit compaction even without an explicit event.
    56→
    57→**Session handoff** occurs when Claude starts a new session ID (observed after exiting plan mode). Rather than building complex session-linking machinery now, we added a `PreviousSessionID` field on `Event` for future use. The current shadow branch architecture already handles this gracefully — multiple session IDs naturally share the same shadow branch and condense together.
    58→
    59→### Terminology (established in this plan)
    60→- **Step** = saved to shadow branch (`entire/<hash>-<worktreeHash>`) — temporary, per-turn
    61→- **Checkpoint** = persisted to `entire/checkpoints/v1` — permanent, on turn end and git commit
    62→
    63→### Prerequisites
    64→- **ENT-250** (ActionHandler interface for compile-time exhaustive action dispatch) should be completed first. It's self-contained and establishes the pattern we'll extend here.
    65→
    66→---
    67→
    68→## Phase 1: Define Event Types and New Agent Interface
    69→
    70→### 1a. Event types (`cmd/entire/cli/agent/event.go` — new file)
    71→
    72→```go
    73→type EventType int
    74→const (
    75→    SessionStart  EventType = iota  // Agent session begins
    76→    TurnStart                       // User submitted a prompt
    77→    TurnEnd                         // Agent finished responding
    78→    Compaction                      // Agent about to compress context
    79→    SessionEnd                      // Session terminated
    80→    SubagentStart                   // Subagent spawned
    81→    SubagentEnd                     // Subagent completed
    82→)
    83→
    84→type Event struct {
    85→    Type              EventType
    86→    SessionID         string
    87→    PreviousSessionID string     // Non-empty = continuation/handoff
    88→    SessionRef        string     // Path to transcript file
    89→    Prompt            string     // For TurnStart: user's prompt
    90→    Timestamp         time.Time
    91→
    92→    // Subagent-specific
    93→    ToolUseID         string
    94→    SubagentID        string
    95→    ToolInput         json.RawMessage  // Raw tool input (for subagent type/description extraction)
    96→
    97→    // Response — message to show the user via the agent
    98→    ResponseMessage   string
    99→}
   100→```
   101→
   102→### 1b. New `Agent` interface (`cmd/entire/cli/agent/agent.go` — modify)
   103→
   104→Replace the current 17-method interface with a 14-method interface in three clear groups:
   105→
   106→```go
   107→type Agent interface {
   108→    // --- Identity (5 methods, all trivial one-liners) ---
   109→    Name() AgentName
   110→    Type() AgentType
   111→    Description() string
   112→    DetectPresence() (bool, error)
   113→    ProtectedDirs() []string
   114→
   115→    // --- Event Mapping (2 methods, the core contribution surface) ---
   116→    HookNames() []string
   117→    ParseHookEvent(hookName string, stdin io.Reader) (*Event, error)
   118→
   119→    // --- Transcript Operations (7 methods, called by framework during actions) ---
   120→    ReadTranscript(sessionRef string) ([]byte, error)
   121→    GetTranscriptPosition(sessionRef string) (int, error)
   122→    ExtractModifiedFiles(sessionRef string, fromOffset int) (files []string, newOffset int, err error)
   123→    ExtractPrompts(sessionRef string, fromOffset int) ([]string, error)
   124→    ExtractSummary(sessionRef string) (string, error)
   125→    ChunkTranscript(content []byte, maxSize int) ([][]byte, error)
   126→    ReassembleTranscript(chunks [][]byte) ([]byte, error)
   127→}
   128→```
   129→
   130→Methods removed from core interface (moved to optional interfaces or framework):
   131→- `GetHookConfigPath()` → move to `HookInstaller`
   132→- `SupportsHooks()` → inferred from `HookInstaller` implementation
   133→- `ParseHookInput()` → replaced by `ParseHookEvent()`
   134→- `GetSessionID()` → extracted inside `ParseHookEvent()`
   135→- `TransformSessionID()` / `ExtractAgentSessionID()` → internal to agent, used inside `ParseHookEvent()`
   136→- `GetSessionDir()` / `ResolveSessionFile()` / `ReadSession()` / `WriteSession()` / `FormatResumeCommand()` → move to `SessionResumer`
   137→
   138→### 1c. Optional interfaces (keep existing, reorganize)
   139→
   140→```go
   141→// For agents where we can auto-install hooks
   142→type HookInstaller interface {
   143→    Agent
   144→    GetHookConfigPath() string
   145→    InstallHooks(localDev, force bool) (int, error)

   150→
   151→// For agents that support session resumption (rewind --resume)

   160→
   161→// For agents without hooks — file-watching based

   166→}
   167→```

   171→### 1d. Agent-specific concerns
   172→
   173→**Transcript flush (Claude-specific):** Add an optional interface:
   174→```go
   175→// TranscriptPreparer is called before ReadTranscript to handle agent-specific
   176→// flush/sync requirements (e.g., Claude Code's async transcript writing).
   177→type TranscriptPreparer interface {
   178→    Agent
   179→    PrepareTranscript(sessionRef string) error
   180→}
   181→```
   182→The framework calls this before `ReadTranscript()` if implemented. Claude implements it with the sentinel polling logic from `waitForTranscriptFlush()`.

   201→
   202→## Phase 2: Build Generic Lifecycle Dispatcher
   203→
   204→### 2a. Pre-prompt state unification
   205→
   206→Currently there are two separate functions: `CapturePrePromptState()` (Claude, JSONL line counting) and `CaptureGeminiPrePromptState()` (Gemini, JSON message counting). Both capture untracked files + transcript position.
   207→
   208→Unify into a single function that uses the Agent interface:
   209→```go
   210→func CapturePrePromptState(ag agent.Agent, sessionID, sessionRef string) error {
   211→    position, err := ag.GetTranscriptPosition(sessionRef)
   212→    // ... store position + untracked files
   213→}
   214→```
   215→
   216→The `PrePromptState` struct already has both `StepTranscriptStart` (used by Claude) and `StartMessageIndex` (used by Gemini) — unify these into a single `TranscriptOffset int` field.
   217→
   218→### 2b. Generic dispatcher (`cmd/entire/cli/lifecycle.go` — new file)
   219→
   220→Single entry point replacing all per-agent handler logic:
   221→
   222→```go
   223→func DispatchLifecycleEvent(ag agent.Agent, event *agent.Event) error {
   224→    switch event.Type {
   225→    case agent.SessionStart:
   226→        return handleSessionStart(ag, event)
   227→    case agent.TurnStart:
   228→        return handleTurnStart(ag, event)
   229→    case agent.TurnEnd:
   230→        return handleTurnEnd(ag, event)
   231→    case agent.Compaction:
   232→        return handleCompaction(ag, event)
   233→    case agent.SessionEnd:
   234→        return handleSessionEnd(ag, event)
   235→    case agent.SubagentStart:
   236→        return handleSubagentStart(ag, event)
   237→    case agent.SubagentEnd:
   238→        return handleSubagentEnd(ag, event)
   239→    }
   240→}
   241→```
   242→
   243→Each handler function encapsulates the framework logic that's currently duplicated across `commitWithMetadata()` and `handleGeminiAfterAgent()`:
   244→
   245→**`handleTurnEnd(ag, event)`** — the main flow (framework orchestrates, agent provides data):
   246→1. Validate transcript exists
   247→2. If agent implements `TranscriptPreparer`, call `ag.PrepareTranscript()` *(Claude: flush wait)*
   248→3. Create session metadata directory
   249→4. Read transcript via `ag.ReadTranscript(event.SessionRef)` *(agent provides bytes)*
   250→5. Load pre-prompt state (captured on TurnStart)
   251→6. Extract modified files via `ag.ExtractModifiedFiles(sessionRef, offset)` *(agent parses its format)*
   252→7. Extract prompts via `ag.ExtractPrompts(sessionRef, offset)` *(agent parses its format)*
   253→8. Extract summary via `ag.ExtractSummary(sessionRef)` *(agent parses its format)*
   254→9. Detect file changes via `DetectFileChanges()` *(framework, git status)*
   255→10. Filter and normalize paths *(framework)*
   256→11. If no changes: transition phase, clean up, return
   257→12. Create context file *(framework, agent-agnostic)*
   258→13. If agent implements `TokenCalculator`, call `ag.CalculateTokenUsage()` *(optional)*
   259→14. Build `strategy.StepContext`, call `strat.SaveStep()` — **StepSave action**
   260→15. Persist checkpoint to `entire/checkpoints/v1` — **Checkpoint action**
   261→16. Transition session phase (TurnEnd) *(framework, state machine)*
   262→17. Clean up pre-prompt state *(framework)*
   263→
   264→**`handleTurnStart(ag, event)`:**
   265→1. Capture pre-prompt state using `ag.GetTranscriptPosition()`
   266→2. If strategy implements `SessionInitializer`, call `InitializeSession()`
   267→
   268→**`handleSessionStart(ag, event)`:**
   269→1. Check concurrent sessions
   270→2. Output hook response message
   271→3. Fire `EventSessionStart` on state machine
   272→
   273→**`handleSessionEnd(ag, event)`:**
   274→1. Mark session ended via `markSessionEnded()`
   275→
   276→**`handleCompaction(ag, event)`:**
   277→1. Same as `handleTurnEnd()` but also resets transcript offset in session state
   278→
   279→**`handleSubagentStart(ag, event)`:**
   280→1. Capture pre-task state
   281→
   282→**`handleSubagentEnd(ag, event)`:**
   283→1. Extract modified files from subagent transcript
   284→2. Detect file changes
   285→3. Build `TaskStepContext`, call `strat.SaveTaskStep()`
   286→4. Clean up pre-task state
   287→
   288→### 2c. Hook command integration
   289→
   290→Modify `newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging()` in `hook_registry.go` to use the generic dispatcher:
   291→
   292→```go
   293→RunE: func(_ *cobra.Command, _ []string) error {
   294→    // ... existing setup (repo check, logging, agent resolution) ...
   295→
   296→    event, err := ag.ParseHookEvent(hookName, os.Stdin)
   297→    if err != nil {
   298→        return err
   299→    }
   300→    if event == nil {
   301→        return nil  // Agent says: no lifecycle action needed
   302→    }
   303→    return DispatchLifecycleEvent(ag, event)
   304→}
   305→```
   306→
   307→This replaces the entire `hookRegistry` map + `GetHookHandler()` lookup + per-agent `init()` registration.

   316→## Phase 3: Migrate Agents

   318→### 3a. Migrate Claude Code
   319→
   320→Implement `ParseHookEvent()` on `ClaudeCodeAgent`:
   321→
   322→```go
   323→func (a *ClaudeCodeAgent) ParseHookEvent(hookName string, stdin io.Reader) (*Event, error) {
   324→    switch hookName {
   325→    case HookNameSessionStart:
   326→        return parseClaudeSessionStart(stdin)
   327→    case HookNameUserPromptSubmit:
   328→        return parseClaudeTurnStart(stdin)
   329→    case HookNameStop:
   330→        return parseClaudeTurnEnd(stdin)
   331→    case HookNameSessionEnd:
   332→        return parseClaudeSessionEnd(stdin)
   333→    case HookNamePreTask:
   334→        return parseClaudeSubagentStart(stdin)
   335→    case HookNamePostTask:
   336→        return parseClaudeSubagentEnd(stdin)
   337→    case HookNamePostTodo:
   338→        return nil, nil  // Agent-specific: handled outside generic dispatcher
   339→    }
   340→    return nil, nil
   341→}
   342→```

   359→func (a *GeminiCLIAgent) ParseHookEvent(hookName string, stdin io.Reader) (*Event, error) {
   360→    switch hookName {

   460→## Migration Strategy
   461→
   462→Phases 1-2 are additive — the new interface and dispatcher can coexist with the old handler system. During Phase 3, we migrate one agent at a time:
   463→
   464→1. Add `ParseHookEvent()` to Claude Code agent, update dispatcher to use it
   465→2. Verify all Claude Code tests pass
   466→3. Delete `hooks_claudecode_handlers.go`
   467→4. Repeat for Gemini CLI
   468→5. Delete old registry code
   469→
   470→This ensures we never have a broken intermediate state.

   474→## Verification
   475→
   476→After each phase:
   477→```bash
   478→mise run fmt && mise run lint && mise run test:ci
   479→```
   480→
   481→End-to-end validation:
   482→1. `entire enable --agent claude-code` → hooks install correctly
   483→2. Start a Claude Code session → SessionStart event dispatched, banner shown
   484→3. Submit a prompt → TurnStart captures pre-prompt state
   485→4. Agent completes → TurnEnd saves step + checkpoint
   486→5. `git commit` → condensation works
   487→6. `entire rewind` → rewind points listed correctly
   488→7. Repeat steps 1-6 for Gemini CLI
   489→
   490→Integration tests in `cmd/entire/cli/integration_test/` cover the strategy-level flows. Add new integration tests for the lifecycle dispatcher.
   491→
   492→---
   493→
   494→## Addendum: Validation Against Third-Party Agent Contributions
   495→
   496→We reviewed open PRs for four third-party agents (OpenCode, Codex CLI, Pi, Cursor) and cross-referenced each against official documentation and source code. This section documents what we found and what adjustments the plan needs.
   497→
   498→### Validation confirms the core design
   499→
   500→The strongest signal from the PR review is the **massive duplication of framework logic**. Every contributed agent re-implements the same ~300-line handler pattern: pre-prompt state capture, transcript copying, file change detection, path normalization, context file creation, `strategy.SaveChanges()`, session state transitions. PR #257 (Codex + OpenCode) recognized this independently and extracted a shared `commitAgentSession()` function — which is essentially a partial version of Phase 2's `DispatchLifecycleEvent`. The plan's inversion ("framework calls agent for data") would eliminate ~1500 lines of duplicated handler code across these agents.
   501→
   502→The 7 event types (SessionStart, TurnStart, TurnEnd, Compaction, SessionEnd, SubagentStart, SubagentEnd) are sufficient for all four agents. No agent needs events outside this set. Agents with fewer hooks (e.g., Codex with only TurnEnd today) naturally get degraded functionality — no pre-prompt state capture means git-status-only file detection — which is an acceptable trade-off.
   503→
   504→### Adjustment 1: Framework-level hook input normalization
   505→
   506→**Problem**: Codex CLI delivers its `notify` payload as the **last argv element**, not via stdin. The Rust source (`codex-rs/hooks/src/user_notification.rs`) explicitly sets `stdin(Stdio::null())` and appends JSON via `command.arg(notify_payload)`. PR #337 assumes stdin delivery and would silently fail. PR #257 discovered this and added `GetCurrentHookArgs()` infrastructure.
   507→
   508→**Note**: The Codex team has confirmed that proper hook support (with stdin-based payload delivery and richer lifecycle events beyond just `agent-turn-complete`) is in active development. Once available, this normalization becomes unnecessary for Codex. However, other future agents may have similar delivery mechanisms.
   509→
   510→**Adjustment**: Add input normalization in `newAgentHookVerbCmdWithLogging()` before calling `ParseHookEvent`. If stdin is empty and positional args contain a JSON payload, wrap the last arg into a `bytes.Reader`. This keeps the `Agent` interface clean (`ParseHookEvent` always receives an `io.Reader`) while handling delivery variation at the framework layer:
   511→
   512→```go
   513→RunE: func(cmd *cobra.Command, args []string) error {
   514→    // ... existing setup ...
   515→
   516→    input := normalizeHookInput(os.Stdin, args)
   517→    event, err := ag.ParseHookEvent(hookName, input)
   518→    // ...
   519→}
   520→
   521→// normalizeHookInput handles agents that deliver payloads via argv instead of stdin.
   522→func normalizeHookInput(stdin io.Reader, args []string) io.Reader {
   523→    // Check if stdin has data
   524→    if f, ok := stdin.(*os.File); ok {
   525→        if stat, err := f.Stat(); err == nil && stat.Size() > 0 {
   526→            return stdin
   527→        }
   528→    }
   529→    // Fall back to last positional arg if it looks like JSON
   530→    if len(args) > 0 {
   531→        last := args[len(args)-1]
   532→        if len(last) > 0 && (last[0] == '{' || last[0] == '[') {
   533→            return strings.NewReader(last)
   534→        }
   535→    }
   536→    return stdin
   537→}
   538→```
   539→
   540→### Adjustment 2: Transcript analysis methods — core vs optional
   541→
   542→**Problem**: The plan states "An agent that can't provide transcript access can't meaningfully participate in the checkpoint lifecycle" and makes all 7 transcript methods core. Evidence from PRs and official docs shows this is *almost* true but not quite:
   543→
   544→- **Cursor** passes `transcript_path` in every hook payload (confirmed in [official docs](https://cursor.com/docs/agent/hooks)), but the transcript file format is not publicly documented. Implementers would need to stub the analysis methods until the format is known.
   545→- **OpenCode** (PR #315, #257) treats transcripts as opaque and relies entirely on git status — yet still provides useful checkpoint functionality.
   546→- **Codex** has full structured transcripts (JSONL RolloutItems) but is transitioning its hook system, meaning the transcript path delivery mechanism may change.
   547→
   548→All agents can provide `ReadTranscript` (raw bytes), `ChunkTranscript`, and `ReassembleTranscript` (storage operations). The divergence is in the 4 analysis methods that require format-specific parsing.
   549→
   550→**Adjustment**: Split transcript operations into core (3 methods) and optional (4 methods):
   551→
   552→```go
   553→type Agent interface {
   554→    // ... Identity (5) + Event Mapping (2) unchanged ...
   555→
   556→    // --- Transcript Storage (3 methods, core) ---
   557→    ReadTranscript(sessionRef string) ([]byte, error)
   558→    ChunkTranscript(content []byte, maxSize int) ([][]byte, error)
   559→    ReassembleTranscript(chunks [][]byte) ([]byte, error)
   560→}
   561→
   562→// TranscriptAnalyzer provides format-specific transcript parsing.
   563→// Agents that implement this get richer checkpoints (transcript-derived file lists,
   564→// prompts, summaries). Agents that don't still participate in the checkpoint lifecycle
   565→// via git-status-based file detection and raw transcript storage.
   566→type TranscriptAnalyzer interface {
   567→    Agent
   568→    GetTranscriptPosition(sessionRef string) (int, error)
   569→    ExtractModifiedFiles(sessionRef string, fromOffset int) (files []string, newOffset int, err error)
   570→    ExtractPrompts(sessionRef string, fromOffset int) ([]string, error)
   571→    ExtractSummary(sessionRef string) (string, error)
   572→}
   573→```
   574→
   575→The dispatcher gracefully degrades:
   576→- With `TranscriptAnalyzer`: incremental transcript parsing, transcript-derived file lists, prompt/summary extraction
   577→- Without: git-status-only file detection, no prompt/summary metadata, transcript stored as opaque blob
   578→
   579→This reduces the core `Agent` interface from 14 to 10 methods while keeping the same functionality for agents that implement the full set.
   580→
   581→### Adjustment 3: Event.Metadata for agent-specific state
   582→
   583→**Problem**: Pi has tree-structured conversations with an `activeLeafId` that determines which branch is "active." This is runtime state (not persisted in the JSONL, confirmed via [Pi source](https://github.com/badlogic/pi-mono)). The Pi agent needs to pass this through the framework so it's available on subsequent calls. Cursor passes `is_background_agent` and `composer_mode` that could be useful for checkpoint metadata.
   584→
   585→**Adjustment**: Add a generic metadata field to `Event`:
   586→
   587→```go
   588→type Event struct {
   589→    // ... existing fields ...
   590→
   591→    // Agent-specific metadata the framework stores and passes back on subsequent events.
   592→    // Examples: Pi's activeLeafId, Cursor's is_background_agent.
   593→    Metadata map[string]string
   594→}
   595→```
   596→
   597→The framework stores this in session state and makes it available to subsequent `ParseHookEvent` calls (or transcript method calls) via context. This avoids widening the core interface for agent-specific concerns.
   598→
   599→### Agent-specific notes
   600→
   601→#### OpenCode
   602→
   603→Verified against [official docs](https://opencode.ai/docs/plugins/) and [source](https://github.com/anomalyco/opencode).
   604→
   605→- **Plugin system**: `.opencode/plugins/entire.ts` with named export receiving `{ project, client, $, directory, worktree }` — well-documented, stable API
   606→- **Events**: `session.created`, `session.idle` (deprecated, use `session.status`), `tool.execute.before`, `tool.execute.after` — all confirmed
   607→- **Transcript access**: Via `client.session.messages()` SDK call, exported to temp JSONL — works but adds indirection (OpenCode stores sessions in SQLite, not JSONL files)
   608→- **Event mapping**: SessionStart (`session.created`), TurnEnd (`session.status` type=idle), SubagentStart/End (`tool.execute.before/after`). No TurnStart event exists — pre-prompt state capture not possible, git-status-only file detection
   609→- **Resume**: `opencode --session <id>` (not `--resume`)
   610→- **HookInstaller**: generates plugin file — fits cleanly
   611→
   612→#### Codex CLI
   613→
   614→Verified against [source](https://github.com/openai/codex) and [docs](https://developers.openai.com/codex/config-reference/).
   615→
   616→- **Current hook system**: Single `notify` event (`agent-turn-complete`) delivered via argv with `stdin(Stdio::null())`. Payload is legacy JSON with kebab-case fields (`thread-id`, `turn-id`, `input-messages`, `last-assistant-message`)
   617→- **Proper hook support in development**: The Codex team has confirmed that a richer hook system with stdin-based payload delivery and more lifecycle events is actively being built. Once shipped, Codex would support TurnStart, TurnEnd, SessionStart/End, etc. — mapping cleanly to our event model without the argv normalization workaround
   618→- **Transcript**: JSONL at `~/.codex/sessions/YYYY/MM/DD/rollout-*.jsonl` with typed `RolloutItem` discriminants (`session_meta`, `response_item`, `event_msg`, `compacted`, `turn_context`). Structured `file_change` events exist in the exec stream, and `patch_apply_begin`/`patch_apply_end` in internal EventMsg — no need for heuristic shell command parsing
   619→- **Config**: Both `~/.codex/config.toml` (global) and `.codex/config.toml` (project-local, requires trust) are supported
   620→- **Resume**: `codex resume <id>` (subcommand, not `--resume` flag)
   621→- **HookInstaller**: Modifies TOML `notify` array — fits cleanly
   622→
   623→#### Pi
   624→
   625→Verified against [source](https://github.com/badlogic/pi-mono) and [extension docs](https://github.com/badlogic/pi-mono/blob/main/packages/coding-agent/docs/extensions.md).
   626→
   627→- **Extension system**: `.pi/extensions/entire/index.ts` — auto-discovered, hot-reloaded, TypeScript. 26 total lifecycle events available.
   628→- **Events**: All 9 events used in PR #251 are confirmed real and documented. Richest event coverage of any contributed agent — maps to 6/7 of our event types (everything except Compaction, though `session_compact` exists and could be added)
   629→- **Tree-structured transcripts**: JSONL with `id`/`parentId` pointers forming a tree. `activeLeafId` is runtime state from `SessionManager.getLeafId()`, not persisted. Agent must reconstruct active branch via tree traversal — handled internally in `ExtractModifiedFiles`/`ExtractPrompts`, transparent to framework
   630→- **Resume**: `pi -c` (continue last), `pi -r` (interactive picker), `pi --session <path>` (specific session), or `/resume` in-TUI
   631→- **HookInstaller**: generates extension scaffold — fits cleanly
   632→- **Best-fit agent**: Pi maps most naturally to the TASK.md model. Full lifecycle events, accessible JSONL transcripts, clean extension API.
   633→
   634→#### Cursor
   635→
   636→Verified against [official hooks docs](https://cursor.com/docs/agent/hooks) and [third-party hooks docs](https://cursor.com/docs/agent/third-party-hooks).
   637→
   638→- **Hooks API**: `.cursor/hooks.json` with 20+ event types — richest hook surface of any agent. Includes `sessionStart`, `sessionEnd`, `beforeSubmitPrompt`, `stop`, `preToolUse`, `postToolUse`, `subagentStart`, `subagentStop`, `preCompact`, `afterFileEdit`, and more. Maps to all 7 of our event types including Compaction and Subagent events.
   639→- **Payload delivery**: JSON on stdin, with universal fields on every hook (`conversation_id`, `generation_id`, `model`, `transcript_path`, `workspace_roots`, `user_email`). The `transcript_path` field is significant — Cursor provides the transcript file path in every hook payload.
   640→- **Transcript**: Format of the file at `transcript_path` is not publicly documented. Primary storage is SQLite (`state.vscdb`). The hook-provided transcript file is likely JSONL but this needs verification.
   641→- **Native Claude Code hook loading**: Cursor auto-discovers hooks from `.claude/settings.json` and translates event names (PascalCase → camelCase) and tool names (`Bash` → `Shell`, `Edit` → `Write`). This means `entire enable --agent claude-code` may already work in Cursor without a separate agent — **needs testing**. If the stdin payload format matches Claude's, no Cursor-specific agent is needed. If Cursor sends its own payload format (with `conversation_id`, `cursor_version`, etc.), a Cursor agent or detection layer is required.
   642→- **CLI**: `cursor-agent --resume <id>` (not `cursor --resume`)
   643→- **HookInstaller**: writes `.cursor/hooks.json` — fits cleanly
   644→- **Recommendation**: Before building a Cursor agent, test whether `entire enable --agent claude-code` works in Cursor via the native hook translation. If it does, document it. If not, build a Cursor agent that leverages the full 20+ event hook surface.
   645→
   646→---
   647→
   648→## Caveats
   649→
   650→### Cursor payload format ambiguity
   651→
   652→Cursor's third-party hooks documentation says it loads Claude Code hooks from `.claude/settings.json`, but it's unclear whether the **stdin payload** sent to those hooks matches Claude Code's format or Cursor's native format (which includes extra fields like `conversation_id`, `cursor_version`, `workspace_roots`). If Cursor sends its own format, the Claude Code agent's `ParseHookEvent` would fail to unmarshal the payload. This needs empirical testing before deciding whether a separate Cursor agent is necessary.
   653→
   654→### Codex hook system in transition
   655→
   656→The current Codex `notify` system is minimal (single event, argv delivery, fire-and-forget). A proper hook system with stdin payloads and richer lifecycle events is in active development by the Codex team. Our implementation should target the new hook system rather than building elaborate workarounds for the legacy `notify` mechanism. The argv normalization in Adjustment 1 is a stopgap — once the new hooks ship, Codex would use standard stdin delivery like every other agent.
   657→
   658→### OpenCode `session.idle` deprecation
   659→
   660→OpenCode's `session.idle` event is marked as deprecated in the source code (`packages/opencode/src/session/status.ts`). It still fires alongside `session.status`, but new integrations should use `session.status` and check `event.properties.status.type === "idle"`. The plugin should be updated before merging any OpenCode PR.
   661→
   662→### OpenCode transcript indirection
   663→
   664→OpenCode stores sessions in SQLite, not as filesystem JSONL files. The plugin must export transcripts via the SDK (`client.session.messages()`) to a temp JSONL file. This adds a layer of indirection and means the transcript is a point-in-time snapshot, not a live file. `GetTranscriptPosition` based on JSONL line offsets works, but the offset resets if the plugin re-exports the full transcript (which it does on every hook invocation in PR #341). Incremental offset tracking requires the plugin to append to the existing JSONL file rather than overwriting it.
   665→
   666→### Pi tree-structured transcripts
   667→
   668→Pi's `id`/`parentId` tree model means that naive line-based transcript parsing returns entries from **all branches**, not just the active one. The agent must reconstruct the active branch path by walking `parentId` pointers from the leaf. The `activeLeafId` is runtime state from `SessionManager.getLeafId()` and must be passed from the extension to the CLI (PR #251 does this via the hook payload's `active_leaf_id` field). If the leaf ID is lost (e.g., between sessions), the agent falls back to the last entry in the JSONL, which may be on a stale branch.
   669→
   670→### `TranscriptAnalyzer` degradation path
   671→
   672→When an agent doesn't implement `TranscriptAnalyzer`, the dispatcher must handle:
   673→- **File detection**: git-status-only (compare worktree against pre-prompt untracked baseline). This works well for new/deleted files but can't distinguish "files the agent modified" from "files modified by other processes."
   674→- **Prompts**: Empty — no prompt metadata in checkpoints. The `prompt.txt` file would be omitted or contain only the TurnStart event's `Prompt` field if available.
   675→- **Summary**: Empty — no summary in checkpoint metadata.
   676→- **Transcript position**: No incremental parsing. The full transcript is stored on every step. This increases storage but is functionally correct.
   677→- **Pre-prompt state**: `CapturePrePromptState` can still capture untracked files even without `GetTranscriptPosition`. The `TranscriptOffset` field would be 0, meaning the next `ExtractModifiedFiles` call (if the agent later implements `TranscriptAnalyzer`) processes the full transcript.
   678→

<system-reminder>
Whenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

```

Perfect! Now let me create a comprehensive summary of the architecture I've explored.

## Summary: Agent and Hook Architecture in CLI Codebase

Based on my thorough exploration of the codebase, here's a detailed summary of the current architecture:

---

### 1. **Agent Interface** (`cmd/entire/cli/agent/agent.go`)

The `Agent` interface is the core abstraction for integrating different coding agents. Key methods:

#### Core Methods:
- **`Name()`** → Returns agent registry key (e.g., "claude-code", "gemini")
- **`Type()`** → Returns agent type identifier (e.g., AgentTypeClaudeCode) for metadata/trailers
- **`Description()`** → Human-readable description for UI
- **`DetectPresence()`** → Checks if agent is configured in the repo
- **`GetHookConfigPath()`** → Path to hook config file (empty if none)
- **`SupportsHooks()`** → Boolean indicating hook support
- **`ParseHookInput()`** → Parses hook callback input from stdin
- **`GetSessionID()`** → Extracts session ID from hook input
- **`ProtectedDirs()`** → Directories never to modify/delete during rewind (e.g., [".claude"])
- **`GetSessionDir()`** → Where agent stores session data (e.g., ~/.claude/projects/<sanitized-path>)
- **`ResolveSessionFile()`** → Maps session ID to file path (Claude: `<id>.jsonl`, Gemini: `session-<date>-<shortid>.json`)
- **`ReadSession()`** → Reads session data from agent storage
- **`WriteSession()`** → Writes session data for resumption
- **`FormatResumeCommand()`** → Returns command to resume a session

#### Optional Interfaces:
- **`HookSupport`** → InstallHooks(), UninstallHooks(), AreHooksInstalled(), GetSupportedHooks()
- **`HookHandler`** → GetHookNames() - defines which hook verbs the agent supports
- **`FileWatcher`** → For agents without hooks (Aider): GetWatchPaths(), OnFileChange()
- **`TranscriptAnalyzer`** → GetTranscriptPosition(), ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset() - for incremental parsing
- **`TranscriptChunker`** → ChunkTranscript(), ReassembleTranscript() - for large transcripts

---

### 2. **Hook Types and Input** (`cmd/entire/cli/agent/types.go`)

#### HookType Constants:
```go
HookSessionStart HookType = "session_start"
HookSessionEnd HookType = "session_end"
HookUserPromptSubmit HookType = "user_prompt_submit"
HookStop HookType = "stop"
HookPreToolUse HookType = "pre_tool_use"
HookPostToolUse HookType = "post_tool_use"
```

#### HookInput Structure:
```go
type HookInput struct {
    HookType HookType
    SessionID string
    SessionRef string           // Agent-specific reference (file path, db key)
    Timestamp time.Time
    UserPrompt string           // From UserPromptSubmit hooks
    ToolName, ToolUseID string
    ToolInput, ToolResponse []byte // Raw JSON
    RawData map[string]interface{} // Agent-specific extensions
}
```

#### TokenUsage Structure:
Tracks input/output/cache tokens and API call count per checkpoint (can include subagent tokens).

---

### 3. **Hook Registry** (`cmd/entire/cli/hook_registry.go`)

#### Registry Design:
- Global map: `hookRegistry[agentName][hookName] → HookHandlerFunc`
- Handler signature: `type HookHandlerFunc func() error`
- Registration happens in `init()` - no parameters passed; handlers read from stdin/environment
- `GetHookHandler()` returns handler for given agent+hook pair, or nil if not found
- `GetCurrentHookAgent()` retrieves the agent for the currently executing hook (set by command context)

#### Hook Command Structure:
- Root: `entire hooks`
- Agent level: `entire hooks <agent-name>`
- Hook level: `entire hooks <agent-name> <hook-name>`

Each agent's hooks are discovered dynamically via `HookHandler.GetHookNames()`.

---

### 4. **Claude Code Handler** (`cmd/entire/cli/hooks_claudecode_handlers.go`)

Key handler functions:

- **`captureInitialState()`** (UserPromptSubmit) → Captures pre-prompt state, calls strategy's SessionInitializer
- **`commitWithMetadata()`** (Stop) → Main checkpoint creation logic:
  - Copies transcript to session metadata directory
  - Loads pre-prompt state (captured at turn start)
  - Detects file changes (modified, new, deleted)
  - Creates context.md with commit message, prompts, summary
  - Builds SaveContext and delegates to strategy.SaveChanges()
  - Updates session state (for auto-commit strategy)
  - Transitions session ACTIVE → IDLE
  
- **`handleClaudeCodePreTask()`** (PreToolUse[Task]) → Captures pre-task state before subagent executes
- **`handleClaudeCodePostTask()`** (PostToolUse[Task]) → Creates task checkpoint when subagent completes
- **`handleClaudeCodePostTodo()`** (PostToolUse[TodoWrite]) → Creates incremental checkpoints during task execution

Helper functions:
- `parseAndLogHookInput()` → Standardized parsing and logging
- `transitionSessionTurnEnd()` → Fires EventTurnEnd state machine event
- `markSessionEnded()` → Transitions session to ENDED phase
- `waitForTranscriptFlush()` → Polls for hook_progress sentinel to ensure transcript is flushed

---

### 5. **Gemini CLI Handler** (`cmd/entire/cli/hooks_geminicli_handlers.go`)

Similar patterns to Claude, but with Gemini-specific differences:

- **`handleGeminiBeforeAgent()`** (equivalent to UserPromptSubmit) → Captures pre-prompt state with message count
- **`handleGeminiAfterAgent()`** (equivalent to Stop) → Creates checkpoint:
  - Extracts prompts, summary, modified files from Gemini's JSON transcript
  - Detects file changes
  - Calculates token usage (Gemini-specific: cache_read_tokens, api_call_count)
  - Calls strategy.SaveChanges()
  - Transitions ACTIVE → IDLE

- **`handleGeminiSessionEnd()`** → Marks session as ended
- Other handlers (**BeforeTool**, **AfterTool**, **BeforeModel**, **AfterModel**, **BeforeToolSelection**, **PreCompress**, **Notification**) → Mostly logging for now, framework for future use

Helper functions:
- `parseGeminiSessionEnd()` → Extracts session context
- `setupGeminiSessionDir()` → Creates session directory and copies transcript
- `extractGeminiMetadata()` → Pulls prompts/summary/files from JSON
- `commitGeminiSession()` → Delegates to strategy.SaveChanges()

---

### 6. **Strategy Interface** (`cmd/entire/cli/strategy/strategy.go`)

#### Core Methods:
- **`SaveChanges(ctx SaveContext)`** → Main checkpoint creation, called on Stop
- **`SaveTaskCheckpoint(ctx TaskCheckpointContext)`** → Creates task checkpoint (subagent completion)
- **`GetRewindPoints(limit)`** → Returns available rewind points
- **`Rewind(point)`** → Restores to checkpoint
- **`CanRewind()`** → Checks if rewind is currently possible
- **`PreviewRewind(point)`** → Returns what files will be modified/deleted
- **`GetTaskCheckpoint(point)` / `GetTaskCheckpointTranscript(point)`** → Retrieve task checkpoint data
- **`GetSessionInfo()`** → Info for linking commits
- **`EnsureSetup()`** → Installs missing pieces (hooks, gitignore, metadata branch)

#### SaveContext Structure:
```go
type SaveContext struct {
    SessionID, CommitMessage, TranscriptPath string
    ModifiedFiles, NewFiles, DeletedFiles []string
    MetadataDir, MetadataDirAbs string
    AuthorName, AuthorEmail string
    AgentType agent.AgentType
    StepTranscriptIdentifier string  // UUID when step started
    StepTranscriptStart int          // Line count when step started
    TokenUsage *agent.TokenUsage
}
```

#### TaskCheckpointContext Structure:
```go
type TaskCheckpointContext struct {
    SessionID, ToolUseID, AgentID string
    ModifiedFiles, NewFiles, DeletedFiles []string
    TranscriptPath, SubagentTranscriptPath string
    CheckpointUUID string
    IsIncremental bool
    IncrementalSequence int  // Checkpoint sequence number
    IncrementalType string   // Tool name: "TodoWrite", "Edit", etc.
    IncrementalData json.RawMessage
    SubagentType, TaskDescription, TodoContent string
    AuthorName, AuthorEmail string
    AgentType agent.AgentType
}
```

#### Optional Interfaces:
- **`SessionInitializer`** → InitializeSession() - called at turn start to initialize session state
- **`PrepareCommitMsgHandler`** → PrepareCommitMsg() - git hook integration
- **`PostCommitHandler`** → PostCommit() - triggers after commit
- **`CommitMsgHandler`** → CommitMsg() - validates final message
- **`PrePushHandler`** → PrePush() - push side branches alongside user push
- **`TurnEndHandler`** → HandleTurnEnd() - finalize work when turn ends (ACTIVE → IDLE)
- **`LogsOnlyRestorer`** → RestoreLogsOnly() - restore logs without file state
- **`SessionResetter`** → Reset() - clean up shadow branches
- **`SessionCondenser`** → CondenseSessionByID() - force-condense session
- **`ConcurrentSessionChecker`** → CountOtherActiveSessionsWithCheckpoints()
- **`SessionSource`** → GetAdditionalSessions() - provide sessions beyond entire/checkpoints/v1

---

### 7. **Session Phase State Machine** (`cmd/entire/cli/session/phase.go`)

#### Phases:
- `PhaseIdle` - No agent active
- `PhaseActive` - Agent actively working on prompt
- `PhaseEnded` - Session explicitly stopped

#### Events:
```go
EventTurnStart    // Agent begins working on prompt
EventTurnEnd      // Agent finishes its turn
EventGitCommit    // Git commit was made (PostCommit hook)
EventSessionStart // Session process started
EventSessionStop  // Session explicitly stopped
```

#### Actions (side effects to perform):
```go
ActionCondense                  // Condense session to permanent storage
ActionCondenseIfFilesTouched    // Condense only if files were modified
ActionDiscardIfNoFiles          // Discard session if no files touched
ActionWarnStaleSession          // Warn about stale session
ActionClearEndedAt              // Clear EndedAt timestamp
ActionUpdateLastInteraction     // Update LastInteractionTime
```

#### Key Transitions:
- `IDLE + TurnStart → ACTIVE` (with UpdateLastInteraction)
- `ACTIVE + TurnEnd → IDLE` (with UpdateLastInteraction)
- `IDLE/ACTIVE + GitCommit → same phase` (with Condense + UpdateLastInteraction)
- `ACTIVE + SessionStop → ENDED` (with UpdateLastInteraction)
- `ENDED + GitCommit [files] → ENDED` (with CondenseIfFilesTouched)
- `ENDED + GitCommit [no files] → ENDED` (with DiscardIfNoFiles)
- `ENDED + TurnStart → ACTIVE` (with ClearEndedAt + UpdateLastInteraction)

#### Transition Function:
Pure function: `Transition(currentPhase Phase, event Event, ctx TransitionContext) → TransitionResult`
- No side effects - returns (NewPhase, []Action)
- Normalizes empty/unknown phases to PhaseIdle for backward compatibility
- Handles context flags: HasFilesTouched, IsRebaseInProgress

---

### 8. **PrePromptState** (`cmd/entire/cli/state.go`)

Captures state before a user prompt is submitted:

```go
type PrePromptState struct {
    SessionID string
    Timestamp string
    UntrackedFiles []string
    StartMessageIndex int                 // For Gemini: message count at start
    LastTranscriptIdentifier string       // UUID (Claude) or message ID (Gemini)
    StepTranscriptStart int                // Line count (Claude) or message count (Gemini)
}
```

Functions:
- **`CapturePrePromptState(sessionID, transcriptPath)`** → Captures untracked files and transcript position (Claude)
- **`CaptureGeminiPrePromptState(sessionID, transcriptPath)`** → Gemini-specific version (reads JSON message count)
- **`LoadPrePromptState(sessionID)`** → Loads previously captured state
- **`CleanupPrePromptState(sessionID)`** → Removes state file

PreTaskState similar for task execution capture.

---

### 9. **Claude Code Agent** (`cmd/entire/cli/agent/claudecode/claude.go`)

Implementation highlights:

- **Name/Type**: "claude-code" / AgentTypeClaudeCode
- **SessionDir**: `~/.claude/projects/<sanitized-repo-path>/`
- **Hook Config**: `.claude/settings.json`
- **Transcript Format**: JSONL (one JSON object per line)
- **Protected Dirs**: [".claude"]

Methods:
- **`ParseHookInput()`** → Parses Claude's hook JSON format
- **`ReadSession()`** → Reads JSONL transcript, parses to extract modified files
- **`WriteSession()`** → Writes JSONL bytes
- **`GetTranscriptPosition()`** → Counts lines in JSONL file (lightweight, no JSON parsing)
- **`ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(startLine)`** → Parses only NEW lines since last checkpoint
- **`ChunkTranscript() / ReassembleTranscript()`** → JSONL chunking at line boundaries
- **`TruncateAtUUID()`** → Truncates transcript at given message UUID (for rewind)
- **`FindCheckpointUUID()`** → Finds UUID of tool_result message for task rewind

---

### 10. **Gemini CLI Agent** (`cmd/entire/cli/agent/geminicli/gemini.go`)

Implementation highlights:

- **Name/Type**: "gemini" / AgentTypeGemini
- **SessionDir**: `~/.gemini/tmp/<sanitized-repo-path>/chats/`
- **Hook Config**: `.gemini/settings.json`
- **Transcript Format**: JSON with `{"messages": [...]}`
- **Protected Dirs**: [".gemini"]

Methods:
- **`ParseHookInput()`** → Parses Gemini's hook JSON format
- **`ReadSession()`** → Reads JSON transcript, extracts modified files
- **`WriteSession()`** → Writes JSON bytes
- **`GetTranscriptPosition()`** → Counts messages in JSON array
- **`ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(startIndex)`** → Parses messages from index onward, extracts files from tool calls
- **`ChunkTranscript() / ReassembleTranscript()`** → Distributes messages across chunks, preserves JSON structure
- **`CalculateTokenUsageFromFile()`** → Extracts token counts from transcript starting at message index

---

### 11. **Hook Flow Summary**

```
Agent lifecycle event (e.g., user submits prompt)
    ↓
Hook invoked: entire hooks <agent-name> <hook-name>
    ↓
Command parses args, calls PersistentPreRunE (init logging)
    ↓
Sets currentHookAgentName
    ↓
Looks up handler: GetHookHandler(agentName, hookName)
    ↓
Handler executes (reads from stdin, calls agent.ParseHookInput)
    ↓
Handler may:
  - Capture state (PrePromptState, PreTaskState)
  - Call strategy.SaveChanges() or strategy.SaveTaskCheckpoint()
  - Call strategy.TransitionAndLog() to update session phase
  - Transition session state machine
    ↓
Handler returns error (or SilentError for custom messages)
    ↓
PersistentPostRunE cleans up logging
    ↓
Exit code indicates success/failure
```

---

### 12. **Key Design Patterns**

1. **Agent Abstraction**: Each agent implements the same interface, CLI doesn't care about implementation details

2. **Hook Dispatch**: Registry-based, agents define hook names, CLI maps names to handlers in central registry

3. **State Capture Tiers**:
   - **PrePromptState**: What files existed before prompt
   - **Session state** (.git/entire-sessions/): Long-lived session metadata
   - **Metadata** (.entire/metadata/ or entire/checkpoints/v1): Permanent storage

4. **Transcript Position Tracking**:
   - Claude: Line count + UUID (for safe truncation)
   - Gemini: Message count + message ID
   - Used to parse only NEW lines/messages on subsequent checkpoints

5. **Token Usage**: Agent-agnostic structure, populated by the agent that created the checkpoint

6. **Session Phase Machine**: Pure state transitions, side effects dispatched separately via ActionHandler

7. **Strategy Pattern**: Multiple strategies (manual-commit, auto-commit) implement same interface, different checkpoint/metadata storage

8. **File Change Detection**: CLI responsibility, uses git status at repo root (not cwd) to avoid filtering out sibling directories

---

This architecture enables plugging in new agents (Cursor, Aider, etc.) without changing CLI core logic - just implement the Agent interface and register it!

     1→// Package agent provides interfaces and types for integrating with coding agents.
     2→// It abstracts agent-specific behavior (hooks, log parsing, session storage) so that
     3→// the same Strategy implementations can work with any coding agent.
     4→package agent
     5→
     6→import (
     7→	"io"
     8→)
     9→
    10→// Agent defines the interface for interacting with a coding agent.
    11→// Each agent implementation (Claude Code, Cursor, Aider, etc.) converts its
    12→// native format to the normalized types defined in this package.
    13→type Agent interface {
    14→	// Name returns the agent registry key (e.g., "claude-code", "gemini")
    15→	Name() AgentName
    16→
    17→	// Type returns the agent type identifier (e.g., "Claude Code", "Gemini CLI")
    18→	// This is stored in metadata and trailers.
    19→	Type() AgentType
    20→
    21→	// Description returns a human-readable description for UI
    22→	Description() string
    23→
    24→	// DetectPresence checks if this agent is configured in the repository
    25→	DetectPresence() (bool, error)
    26→
    27→	// GetHookConfigPath returns path to hook config file (empty if none)
    28→	GetHookConfigPath() string
    29→
    30→	// SupportsHooks returns true if agent supports lifecycle hooks
    31→	SupportsHooks() bool
    32→
    33→	// ParseHookInput parses hook callback input from stdin
    34→	ParseHookInput(hookType HookType, reader io.Reader) (*HookInput, error)
    35→
    36→	// GetSessionID extracts session ID from hook input
    37→	GetSessionID(input *HookInput) string
    38→
    39→	// ProtectedDirs returns repo-root-relative directories that should never be
    40→	// modified or deleted during rewind or other destructive operations.
    41→	// Examples: [".claude"] for Claude, [".gemini"] for Gemini.
    42→	ProtectedDirs() []string
    43→
    44→	// GetSessionDir returns where agent stores session data for this repo.
    45→	// Examples:
    46→	//   Claude: ~/.claude/projects/<sanitized-repo-path>/
    47→	//   Aider: current working directory (returns repoPath)
    48→	//   Cursor: ~/Library/Application Support/Cursor/User/globalStorage/
    49→	GetSessionDir(repoPath string) (string, error)
    50→
    51→	// ResolveSessionFile returns the path to the session transcript file for a given
    52→	// agent session ID. Agents use different naming conventions:
    53→	//   Claude: <sessionDir>/<id>.jsonl
    54→	//   Gemini: <sessionDir>/session-<date>-<shortid>.json (searches for existing file)
    55→	// If no existing file is found, returns a sensible default path.
    56→	ResolveSessionFile(sessionDir, agentSessionID string) string
    57→
    58→	// ReadSession reads session data from agent's storage.
    59→	// Handles different formats: JSONL (Claude), SQLite (Cursor), Markdown (Aider)
    60→	ReadSession(input *HookInput) (*AgentSession, error)
    61→
    62→	// WriteSession writes session data for resumption.
    63→	// Agent handles format conversion (JSONL, SQLite, etc.)
    64→	WriteSession(session *AgentSession) error
    65→
    66→	// FormatResumeCommand returns command to resume a session
    67→	FormatResumeCommand(sessionID string) string
    68→}
    69→
    70→// HookSupport is implemented by agents with lifecycle hooks.
    71→// This optional interface allows agents like Claude Code and Cursor to
    72→// install and manage hooks that notify Entire of agent events.
    73→type HookSupport interface {
    74→	Agent
    75→
    76→	// InstallHooks installs agent-specific hooks.
    77→	// If localDev is true, hooks point to local development build.
    78→	// If force is true, removes existing Entire hooks before installing.
    79→	// Returns the number of hooks installed.
    80→	InstallHooks(localDev bool, force bool) (int, error)
    81→
    82→	// UninstallHooks removes installed hooks
    83→	UninstallHooks() error
    84→
    85→	// AreHooksInstalled checks if hooks are currently installed
    86→	AreHooksInstalled() bool
    87→
    88→	// GetSupportedHooks returns the hook types this agent supports
    89→	GetSupportedHooks() []HookType
    90→}
    91→
    92→// HookHandler is implemented by agents that define their own hook vocabulary.
    93→// Each agent defines its own hook names (verbs) which become subcommands
    94→// under `entire hooks <agent>`. The actual handling is done by handlers
    95→// registered in the CLI package to avoid circular dependencies.
    96→//
    97→// This allows different agents to have completely different hook vocabularies
    98→// (e.g., Claude Code has "stop", Cursor might have "completion").
    99→type HookHandler interface {
   100→	Agent
   101→
   102→	// GetHookNames returns the hook verbs this agent supports.
   103→	// These are the subcommand names that will appear under `entire hooks <agent>`.
   104→	// e.g., ["stop", "user-prompt-submit", "pre-task", "post-task", "post-todo"]
   105→	GetHookNames() []string
   106→}
   107→
   108→// FileWatcher is implemented by agents that use file-based detection.
   109→// Agents like Aider that don't support hooks can use file watching
   110→// to detect session activity.
   111→type FileWatcher interface {
   112→	Agent
   113→
   114→	// GetWatchPaths returns paths to watch for session changes
   115→	GetWatchPaths() ([]string, error)
   116→
   117→	// OnFileChange handles a detected file change and returns session info
   118→	OnFileChange(path string) (*SessionChange, error)
   119→}
   120→
   121→// TranscriptAnalyzer is implemented by agents that support transcript analysis.
   122→// This allows agent-agnostic detection of work done between checkpoints.
   123→type TranscriptAnalyzer interface {
   124→	Agent
   125→
   126→	// GetTranscriptPosition returns the current position (length) of a transcript.
   127→	// For JSONL formats (Claude Code), this is the line count.
   128→	// For JSON formats (Gemini CLI), this is the message count.
   129→	// Returns 0 if the file doesn't exist or is empty.
   130→	// Use this to efficiently check if the transcript has grown since last checkpoint.
   131→	GetTranscriptPosition(path string) (int, error)
   132→
   133→	// ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset extracts files modified since a given offset.
   134→	// For JSONL formats (Claude Code), offset is the starting line number.
   135→	// For JSON formats (Gemini CLI), offset is the starting message index.
   136→	// Returns:
   137→	//   - files: list of file paths modified by the agent (from Write/Edit tools)
   138→	//   - currentPosition: the current position (line count or message count)
   139→	//   - error: any error encountered during reading
   140→	ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(path string, startOffset int) (files []string, currentPosition int, err error)
   141→}
   142→
   143→// TranscriptChunker is implemented by agents that support transcript chunking.
   144→// This allows agents to split large transcripts into chunks for storage (GitHub has
   145→// a 100MB blob limit) and reassemble them when reading.
   146→type TranscriptChunker interface {
   147→	Agent
   148→
   149→	// ChunkTranscript splits a transcript into chunks if it exceeds maxSize.
   150→	// Returns a slice of chunks. If the transcript fits in one chunk, returns single-element slice.
   151→	// The chunking is format-aware: JSONL splits at line boundaries, JSON splits message arrays.
   152→	ChunkTranscript(content []byte, maxSize int) ([][]byte, error)
   153→
   154→	// ReassembleTranscript combines chunks back into a single transcript.
   155→	// Handles format-specific reassembly (JSONL concatenation, JSON message merging).
   156→	ReassembleTranscript(chunks [][]byte) ([]byte, error)
   157→}
   158→

     5→// HookType represents agent lifecycle events
     6→type HookType string
     7→
     8→const (
     9→	HookSessionStart     HookType = "session_start"
    10→	HookSessionEnd       HookType = "session_end"
    11→	HookUserPromptSubmit HookType = "user_prompt_submit"
    12→	HookStop             HookType = "stop"
    13→	HookPreToolUse       HookType = "pre_tool_use"
    14→	HookPostToolUse      HookType = "post_tool_use"
    15→)
    16→
    17→// HookInput contains normalized data from hook callbacks
    18→type HookInput struct {
    19→	HookType  HookType
    20→	SessionID string
    21→	// SessionRef is an agent-specific session reference (file path, db key, etc.)
    22→	SessionRef string
    23→	Timestamp  time.Time
    24→
    25→	// UserPrompt is the user's prompt text (from UserPromptSubmit hooks)
    26→	UserPrompt string
    27→
    28→	// Tool-specific fields (PreToolUse/PostToolUse)
    29→	ToolName     string
    30→	ToolUseID    string
    31→	ToolInput    []byte // Raw JSON
    32→	ToolResponse []byte // Raw JSON (PostToolUse only)
    33→
    34→	// RawData preserves agent-specific data for extension
    35→	RawData map[string]interface{}
    36→}
    37→
    38→// SessionChange represents detected session activity (for FileWatcher)
    39→type SessionChange struct {
    40→	SessionID  string
    41→	SessionRef string
    42→	EventType  HookType
    43→	Timestamp  time.Time
    44→}
    45→
    46→// TokenUsage represents aggregated token usage for a checkpoint.
    47→// This is agent-agnostic and can be populated by any agent that tracks token usage.
    48→type TokenUsage struct {
    49→	// InputTokens is the number of input tokens (fresh, not from cache)
    50→	InputTokens int `json:"input_tokens"`
    51→	// CacheCreationTokens is tokens written to cache (billable at cache write rate)
    52→	CacheCreationTokens int `json:"cache_creation_tokens"`
    53→	// CacheReadTokens is tokens read from cache (discounted rate)
    54→	CacheReadTokens int `json:"cache_read_tokens"`
    55→	// OutputTokens is the number of output tokens generated
    56→	OutputTokens int `json:"output_tokens"`
    57→	// APICallCount is the number of API calls made
    58→	APICallCount int `json:"api_call_count"`
    59→	// SubagentTokens contains token usage from spawned subagents (if any)
    60→	SubagentTokens *TokenUsage `json:"subagent_tokens,omitempty"`
    61→}

     3→import (
     4→	"fmt"
     5→	"slices"
     6→	"sync"
     7→)
     8→
     9→var (
    10→	registryMu sync.RWMutex
    11→	registry   = make(map[AgentName]Factory)
    12→)
    13→
    14→// Factory creates a new agent instance
    15→type Factory func() Agent
    16→
    17→// Register adds an agent factory to the registry.
    18→// Called from init() in each agent implementation.
    19→func Register(name AgentName, factory Factory) {
    20→	registryMu.Lock()
    21→	defer registryMu.Unlock()
    22→	registry[name] = factory
    23→}
    24→
    25→// Get retrieves an agent by name.
    26→//
    27→
    28→func Get(name AgentName) (Agent, error) {
    29→	registryMu.RLock()
    30→	defer registryMu.RUnlock()
    31→
    32→	factory, ok := registry[name]
    33→	if !ok {
    34→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("unknown agent: %s (available: %v)", name, List())
    35→	}
    36→	return factory(), nil
    37→}
    38→
    39→// List returns all registered agent names in sorted order.
    40→func List() []AgentName {
    41→	registryMu.RLock()
    42→	defer registryMu.RUnlock()
    43→
    44→	names := make([]AgentName, 0, len(registry))
    45→	for name := range registry {
    46→		names = append(names, name)
    47→	}
    48→	slices.Sort(names)
    49→	return names
    50→}
    51→
    52→// Detect attempts to auto-detect which agent is being used.
    53→// Checks each registered agent's DetectPresence method.
    54→//
    55→
    56→func Detect() (Agent, error) {
    57→	registryMu.RLock()
    58→	defer registryMu.RUnlock()
    59→
    60→	for _, factory := range registry {
    61→		ag := factory()
    62→		if present, err := ag.DetectPresence(); err == nil && present {
    63→			return ag, nil
    64→		}
    65→	}
    66→	return nil, fmt.Errorf("no agent detected (available: %v)", List())
    67→}
    68→
    69→// AgentName is the registry key type for agents (e.g., "claude-code", "gemini").
    70→//
    71→//nolint:revive // stuttering is intentional - distinguishes from AgentType when both are used
    72→type AgentName string
    73→
    74→// AgentType is the display name type stored in metadata/trailers (e.g., "Claude Code", "Gemini CLI").
    75→//
    76→//nolint:revive // stuttering is intentional - distinguishes from AgentName when both are used
    77→type AgentType string
    78→
    79→// Agent name constants (registry keys)
    80→const (
    81→	AgentNameClaudeCode AgentName = "claude-code"
    82→	AgentNameGemini     AgentName = "gemini"
    83→)
    84→
    85→// Agent type constants (type identifiers stored in metadata/trailers)
    86→const (
    87→	AgentTypeClaudeCode AgentType = "Claude Code"
    88→	AgentTypeGemini     AgentType = "Gemini CLI"
    89→	AgentTypeUnknown    AgentType = "Agent" // Fallback for backwards compatibility
    90→)
    91→
    92→// DefaultAgentName is the registry key for the default agent.
    93→const DefaultAgentName AgentName = AgentNameClaudeCode
    94→
    95→// GetByAgentType retrieves an agent by its type identifier.
    96→//
    97→// Note: This uses a linear search that instantiates agents until a match is found.
    98→// This is acceptable because:
    99→//   - Agent count is small (~2-20 agents)
   100→//   - Agent factories are lightweight (empty struct allocation)
   101→//   - Called infrequently (commit hooks, rewind, debug commands - not hot paths)
   102→//   - Cost is ~400ns worst case vs milliseconds for I/O operations
   103→//
   104→// Only optimize if agent count exceeds 100 or profiling shows this as a bottleneck.
   105→func GetByAgentType(agentType AgentType) (Agent, error) {
   106→	registryMu.RLock()
   107→	defer registryMu.RUnlock()
   108→
   109→	for _, factory := range registry {
   110→		ag := factory()
   111→		if ag.Type() == agentType {
   112→			return ag, nil
   113→		}
   114→	}
   115→
   116→	return nil, fmt.Errorf("unknown agent type: %s", agentType)
   117→}
   118→
   119→// AllProtectedDirs returns the union of ProtectedDirs from all registered agents.
   120→func AllProtectedDirs() []string {
   121→	// Copy factories under the lock, then release before calling external code.
   122→	registryMu.RLock()
   123→	factories := make([]Factory, 0, len(registry))
   124→	for _, f := range registry {
   125→		factories = append(factories, f)
   126→	}
   127→	registryMu.RUnlock()
   128→
   129→	seen := make(map[string]struct{})
   130→	var dirs []string
   131→	for _, factory := range factories {
   132→		for _, d := range factory().ProtectedDirs() {
   133→			if _, ok := seen[d]; !ok {
   134→				seen[d] = struct{}{}
   135→				dirs = append(dirs, d)
   136→			}
   137→		}
   138→	}
   139→	slices.Sort(dirs)
   140→	return dirs
   141→}
   142→
   143→// Default returns the default agent.
   144→// Returns nil if the default agent is not registered.
   145→//
   146→//nolint:errcheck // Factory pattern returns interface; error is acceptable to ignore for default
   147→func Default() Agent {
   148→	a, _ := Get(DefaultAgentName)
   149→	return a
   150→}
   151→

event types via ParseHookEvent.\ntype EventType int\n\nconst (\n\t// SessionStart indicates the agent session has begun.\n\tSessionStart EventType = iota + 1\n\n\t// TurnStart indicates the user submitted a prompt and the agent is about to work.\n\tTurnStart\n\n\t// TurnEnd indicates the agent finished responding to a prompt.\n\tTurnEnd\n\n\t// Compaction indicates the agent is about to terminated.\n\tSessionEnd\n\n\t// SubagentStart indicates a subagent (task) has been spawned.\n\tSubagentStart\n\n\t// SubagentEnd indicates a subagent (task) has completed.\n\tSubagentEnd\n)\n\n// String returns a human-readable name for the event type.\nfunc (e EventType) String() string {\n\tswitch e {\n\tcase SessionStart:\n\t\treturn \"SessionStart\"\n\tcase TurnStart:\n\t\treturn \"TurnStart\"\n\tcase TurnEnd:\n\t\treturn \"TurnEnd\"\n\tcase Compaction:\n\t\treturn \"Compaction\"\n\tcase SessionEnd:\n\t\treturn \"SessionEnd\"\n\tcase SubagentStart:\n\t\treturn \"SubagentStart\"\n\tcase SubagentEnd:\n\t\treturn \"SubagentEnd\"\n\tdefault:\n\t\treturn \"Unknown\"\n\t}\n}\n\n// Event is a normalized lifecycle event produced by an agent's ParseHookEvent method.\n// The framework dispatcher uses these events to drive checkpoint/session lifecycle actions.\ntype Event struct {\n\t// Type is the kind of lifecycle event.\n\tType EventType\n\n\t// SessionID identifies the agent session.\n\tSessionID string\n\n\t// PreviousSessionID is non-empty when this event represents a session continuation\n\t// or handoff (e.g., Claude starting a new session ID after exiting plan mode).\n\tPreviousSessionID string\n\n\t// SessionRef is an agent-specific reference to the transcript (typically a file path).\n\tSessionRef string\n\n\t// Prompt is the user's prompt text (populated on TurnStart events).\n\tPrompt string\n\n\t// Timestamp is when the event occurred.\n\tTimestamp time.Time\n\n\t// ToolUseID identifies the tool invocation (for SubagentStart/SubagentEnd events).\n\tToolUseID string\n\n\t// SubagentID identifies the subagent instance (for SubagentEnd events).\n\tSubagentID string\n\n\t// ToolInput is the raw tool input JSON (for subagent type/description extraction).\n\tToolInput json.RawMessage\n\n\t// ResponseMessage is an optional message to display to the user via the agent.\n\tResponseMessage string\n\n\t// Metadata holds agent-specific state that the framework stores and makes available\n\t// on subsequent events. Examples: Pi's activeLeafId, Cursor's is_background_agent.\n\tMetadata map[string]string\n}\n"

     1→package agent
     2→
     3→import "time"
     4→
     5→// AgentSession represents a coding session's data.
     6→// Each agent stores data in its native format (JSONL, SQLite, Markdown, etc.)
     7→// and only the originating agent can read/write it.
     8→//
     9→// Design: Sessions are NOT interoperable between agents. A session created by
    10→// Claude Code can only be read/written by Claude Code. This simplifies the
    11→// implementation as we don't need format conversion.
    12→//
    13→//nolint:revive // AgentSession is clearer than Session in context of the package
    14→type AgentSession struct {
    15→	SessionID  string
    16→	AgentName  AgentName
    17→	RepoPath   string
    18→	SessionRef string // Path/reference to session in agent's storage
    19→	StartTime  time.Time
    20→
    21→	// NativeData holds the session content in the agent's native format.
    22→	// Only the originating agent can interpret this data.
    23→	// Examples:
    24→	//   - Claude Code: raw JSONL bytes
    25→	//   - Cursor: serialized SQLite rows
    26→	//   - Aider: Markdown content
    27→	NativeData []byte
    28→
    29→	// Computed fields - populated by the agent when reading
    30→	ModifiedFiles []string
    31→	NewFiles      []string
    32→	DeletedFiles  []string
    33→
    34→	// Optional normalized entries - agents may populate this if needed
    35→	// for operations that benefit from structured access
    36→	Entries []SessionEntry
    37→}
    38→
    39→// SessionEntry represents a single entry in the session
    40→type SessionEntry struct {
    41→	UUID      string
    42→	Type      EntryType
    43→	Timestamp time.Time
    44→	Content   string
    45→
    46→	// Tool-specific fields
    47→	ToolName      string
    48→	ToolInput     interface{}
    49→	ToolOutput    interface{}
    50→	FilesAffected []string
    51→}
    52→
    53→// EntryType categorizes session entries
    54→type EntryType string
    55→
    56→const (
    57→	EntryUser      EntryType = "user"
    58→	EntryAssistant EntryType = "assistant"
    59→	EntryTool      EntryType = "tool"
    60→	EntrySystem    EntryType = "system"
    61→)
    62→
    63→// GetLastUserPrompt returns the last user message content
    64→func (s *AgentSession) GetLastUserPrompt() string {
    65→	for i := len(s.Entries) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
    66→		if s.Entries[i].Type == EntryUser {
    67→			return s.Entries[i].Content
    68→		}
    69→	}
    70→	return ""
    71→}
    72→
    73→// GetLastAssistantResponse returns the last assistant message content
    74→func (s *AgentSession) GetLastAssistantResponse() string {
    75→	for i := len(s.Entries) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
    76→		if s.Entries[i].Type == EntryAssistant {
    77→			return s.Entries[i].Content
    78→		}
    79→	}
    80→	return ""
    81→}
    82→
    83→// TruncateAtUUID returns a new session truncated at the given UUID (inclusive)
    84→func (s *AgentSession) TruncateAtUUID(uuid string) *AgentSession {
    85→	if uuid == "" {
    86→		return s
    87→	}
    88→
    89→	truncated := &AgentSession{
    90→		SessionID:  s.SessionID,
    91→		AgentName:  s.AgentName,
    92→		RepoPath:   s.RepoPath,
    93→		SessionRef: s.SessionRef,
    94→		StartTime:  s.StartTime,
    95→	}
    96→
    97→	for i, entry := range s.Entries {
    98→		truncated.Entries = append(truncated.Entries, entry)
    99→		if entry.UUID == uuid {
   100→			truncated.Entries = s.Entries[:i+1]
   101→			break
   102→		}
   103→	}
   104→
   105→	// Recompute file lists from truncated entries
   106→	fileSet := make(map[string]bool)
   107→	for _, entry := range truncated.Entries {
   108→		for _, f := range entry.FilesAffected {
   109→			if !fileSet[f] {
   110→				fileSet[f] = true
   111→				truncated.ModifiedFiles = append(truncated.ModifiedFiles, f)
   112→			}
   113→		}
   114→	}
   115→
   116→	return truncated
   117→}
   118→
   119→// FindToolResultUUID finds the UUID of the entry containing the tool result
   120→// for the given tool use ID. Returns the UUID and true if found.
   121→func (s *AgentSession) FindToolResultUUID(toolUseID string) (string, bool) {
   122→	for _, entry := range s.Entries {
   123→		if entry.Type == EntryTool && entry.UUID == toolUseID {
   124→			return entry.UUID, true
   125→		}
   126→	}
   127→	return "", false
   128→}
   129→
